# CEAS Rep crowned as the First-Ever Mr. Tourism Olongapo 2025
[Image: CEAS Rep crowned as the First-Ever Mr. Tourism Olongapo 2025]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, October 15, 2025**
“Lord, hindi ako magtatampo kung hindi mo ibibigay sa akin.”
This is the silent cry of Chester Pislaan, a 4th-year student from the College of Education, Arts, and Sciences (CEAS) of Gordon College, before he was crowned as Mr. Tourism Olongapo 2025 during the grand coronation on October 10, 2025, held at the SMX Olongapo Convention Center, SM City Olongapo Central.
Pislaan expressed his plans to use his platform to promote sustainable fashion and empower student workers and breadwinners as the newly crowned Mr. Tourism Olongapo.
“I will focus on informing everyone to refuse fast fashion while also sharing my story, especially with student workers,” he highlighted his advocacy for both environmental and social awareness.
The CEAS Student has a strong will to win, but admitted that things turned upside down when he saw how well the other candidates performed, saying that he’s just like the other contestants— with a heart full of hope and dreams.
“He [Lord] gave me these titles not just for recognition but to expand my reach,” his sentiment after being announced as the winner. He described the moment as both surreal and purposeful, as he connected his earlier achievements as Ginoong Linggo ng Kabataan – Olongapo and Mr. Gordon College Siklab 2025 to a broader mission of service and inspiration.
In addition, Candidate #6 Marc Anthony Acula from the College of Business and Accountancy (CBA), earned the title of 2nd Runner-Up. Candidate #1 Jerichson Martorillas, a student from the College of Education, Arts, and Sciences (CEAS), brought pride to the competition by winning the Best in Talent Award.
Meanwhile, Candidate #7 Blair Ollague, also from CBA, and Candidate #3 Bobby Ken Alfonso Morata from College of Computer Studies (CCS), delivered an exceptional performance that highlighted their confidence, poise, and dedication, making both candidates standout figures of the night. | via Lovely Doroin/The FOREFRONT.

# CEAS yields sixty-eight new LPTs
[Image: CEAS yields sixty-eight new LPTs]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Tuesday, May 12, 2026**
Fourteen elementary and fifty-four secondary education graduates from Gordon College’s (GC) College of Education, Arts, and Sciences (CEAS) finally achieve their sought-after Licensed Professional Teacher (LPT) titles after passing the March 2026 Board Licensure Examination for Professional Teachers (BLEPT) released today, May 12.
Out of 16 BLEPT takers in the elementary level, 14 are now board-certified professional teachers, bringing GC’s passing rate to 87.50% against a 56.03% national passing rate.
Meanwhile, out of 62 takers in the secondary level, 54 have finally claimed their three-letter titles, bringing GC’s passing rate to 87.10% against a 56.10% national passing rate.
Dates for the oathtaking ceremony to induct new professional teachers are yet to be announced as of writing.
As the CEAS conducts its last week of in-house integration review for the BLEPT, graduating preservice teachers await the next licensure exam on September 2026. | via Lance Isaac Leon/The FOREFRONT

# Cosme Y. Sering Library is Now Open
[Image: Cosme Y. Sering Library is Now Open]
[LIMC](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/limc/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Saturday, January 18, 2025**
Today marks a significant milestone for Gordon College, as we celebrate the blessing of the newly renovated Cosme Y. Sering Library. Made possible through the generous donation of the Acevedo Family, this library serves as a vital resource for students, faculty, and the Olongapo City community, promoting learning, collaboration, and access to knowledge.
The Acevedo Family’s generosity exemplifies their commitment to education and community development. This improved facility enhances academic opportunities and enriches the educational experience for students and stakeholders alike.
We extend our deepest gratitude to the Acevedo Family, Hon. Rolen C. Paulino, Former City Mayor, Hon. Rolen C. Paulino Jr., Olongapo City Mayor, Hon/ Cristabelle Marie “Tata” Paulino, Committee on Education Chairperson, Hon. Sarah Lugerna Lipumano-Garcia, City Councilor, Hon. Benjamin G. Cajudo II, Former City Councilor, Dr. Lora Yusi, CHEDROIII Director, Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano, OIC-College President, the City Officials, Board of Trustees, College Officials, College Personnel, Student Leaders, and everyone who contributed to this meaningful endeavor.
With its legacy of excellence and commitment to progress, Gordon College remains steadfast in providing quality education and opportunities for its students and the community. Together, we celebrate the transformative power of education and its enduring impact on society.

# GC presents one thousand students in graduation rites
[Image: GC presents one thousand students in graduation rites]
[Campus](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/campus/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Thursday, July 10, 2025**
Gordon College’s (GC) 23rd Commencement Exercises saw a total of 1,016 passionate graduates walk in the aisle of Subic Bay Exhibition and Convention Center (SBECC) this year, donning their respective togas and sablays.
Amidst heavy rains, Cluster I filled the morning ceremony with 487 graduates from the College of Business and Accountancy (CBA) and Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM).
Meanwhile, 529 students from Cluster II’s College of Allied Health Sciences (CAHS), College of Computer Studies (CCS), and College of Education, Arts, and Sciences (CEAS) graced the stage during the afternoon rites.
The graduates are products of Free Higher Education (FHE) from the institution and taxpayers, who enjoyed it during their tenure.

# Ang Paghatak ni Apolo ng Karwahe sa Dulo ng PaSIKLABan 2025
[Image: Ang Paghatak ni Apolo ng Karwahe sa Dulo ng PaSIKLABan 2025]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Tuesday, September 30, 2025**
OLONGAPO CITY—Ang pagtaklob ng ulap sa nakapapasong araw ay naging tanda ng pagtatapos ng SIKLAB 2025. Sa pagkalansing ng medalya at pagkinang ng tropeyo, umalingawngaw ang sigawan at palakpakan. Kagat-labi ang mga manonood sa antisipasyon kung sino ang magkakamit ng inaasam na pagkapanalo.
Bago parangalan ang magkakampeon sa sagupaan ng mga pating, tigre, wizards, phoenix, at mentor, nagbigay ng natatanging mensahe si Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano na tunay na nagbigay konklusyon sa limang araw na bakbakan sa palarong pampalakasan.
Sa huli, isa lang ang tanong na namutawi: mananatili bang asul ang kulay ng kampeon o mamamayagpag ang bandila ng ibang kolehiyo? | via Kana Maye Dueñas/The FOREFRONT

# GC APERTURA 2025
[Image: GC APERTURA 2025]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Monday, August 11, 2025**
GC APERTURA | Mayor Atty. Rolen C. Paulino Jr. warmly addressed more than 9,000 Gordon College students during the opening of the First Semester for Academic Year 2025–2026 held at the Tapinac Oval Grounds.
In his speech, the Mayor recognized Gordon College as one of Olongapo’s leading educational institutions, commending its role in shaping future professionals.
The event also featured the formal oath-taking of the Supreme Student Council officers for A.Y. 2024–2025, along with the officers of various student organizations and councils, officiated by Mayor Paulino Jr. himself.

# 𝟗 𝐆𝐂𝐈𝐀𝐍𝐒 𝐏𝐀𝐒𝐒𝐄𝐃 𝐂𝐏𝐀𝐋𝐄 𝟐𝟎𝟐𝟓; 𝐆𝐂 𝐑𝐄𝐂𝐎𝐑𝐃𝐒 𝟓𝟔.𝟐𝟓% 𝐏𝐀𝐒𝐒𝐈𝐍𝐆 𝐑𝐀𝐓𝐄
[Image: 𝟗 𝐆𝐂𝐈𝐀𝐍𝐒 𝐏𝐀𝐒𝐒𝐄𝐃 𝐂𝐏𝐀𝐋𝐄 𝟐𝟎𝟐𝟓; 𝐆𝐂 𝐑𝐄𝐂𝐎𝐑𝐃𝐒 𝟓𝟔.𝟐𝟓% 𝐏𝐀𝐒𝐒𝐈𝐍𝐆 𝐑𝐀𝐓𝐄]
[CBA](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/cba/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Saturday, June 7, 2025**
Gordon College (GC) College of Business and Accountrancy (CBA) records 9 new Certified Public Accountant (CPA) in the recently held May 2025 Certified Public Accountant Licensure Exam (CPALE).
The institution aimed an overall perfomance of 56.25%, which surpassed the national rating of 33.11%, with a total of 9 out of 16 examinees joining 3,156 new CPAs in the country.
Out of 13 first time examinees, 8 passed the examination, with 2 considered as conditional. Additionally, among the 3 repeaters, 1 passed while another 1 was classified as conditional.
The licensure examination was administered by the Professional Regulation Comission (PRC) from May 25 to 27 nationwide.
Source: Professional Regulation Commission (PRC)

# Siklab 2025 Told in Gold, Silver, and Bronze
[Image: Siklab 2025 Told in Gold, Silver, and Bronze]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Friday, September 19, 2025**
A story of five teams told across five days, this year’s SIKLAB 2025 championship was a tooth and nail fight for gold, silver, and bronze.
The Tigers clawed their way through the stage.
The Wizards wavered their wands high.
The Phoenix rose with flaming feathers up high
The Sharks splashed onto the podium.
Yet, only one team pulled through.
Clinching forty-four gold, twenty-six silver, and fourteen bronze medals, the Mentors of the College of Education, Arts, and Sciences (CEAS) reigned supreme.
See how the other teams did in battles of skill, physique, teamwork, and wit. | via Lance Isaac Leon /The FOREFRONT

# 3rd Distinguished Alumni Service Award: Call for Nomination 2024
[Image: 3rd Distinguished Alumni Service Award: Call for Nomination 2024]
[Alumni](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/alumni/)
**Wednesday, January 24, 2024**
## THE 3RD GORDON COLLEGE DISTINGUISHED ALUMNI SERVICE AWARD (GC-DASA) FOR THE YEAR 2024
**GENERAL INFORMATION AND GUIDELINES**
Pursuant to Gordon College Alumni Association, Inc. (GCAAI) Board Resolution No. 4, series of 2022, the GCAAI establishes the **DISTINGUISHED ALUMNI SERVICE AWARD (DASA)** to honor Gordon College graduates who have excelled both in their professional and personal careers and who have served as an example to other alumni, students, and the community, both locally and globally. Starting this year, the GCAAI Board of Trustees will recognize the exemplary service and distinguished achievements that brought honor to the Institution and have made significant contributions to society. The GC-DASA recipients will exemplify the core values that the Institution uphold since its establishment: **Exceptional Character, Remarkable Excellence, And Unselfish Service.**
The GC-DASA shall be the highest honor bestowed by the Alumni Association and will be an event to be celebrated annually as part of the GC Founding Anniversary and/or GC Alumni Homecoming.
## GENERAL GUIDELINES:
1. Only graduates of Gordon College (or GC, formerly Olongapo City Colleges) are eligible for the GC-DASA. The nominee shall be a member of the Gordon College Alumni Association, Inc. as confirmed by its Board.
2. Any GC graduate, faculty member, or stakeholders may make nominations for the GC-DASA. Self–nominations are accepted. The nominee shall accomplish the Official Nomination Form to be provided by the GCAAI. No limitation will be placed on the total number of nominations accepted for consideration.
3. The nominee shall have no pending administrative or criminal case upon nomination. He/she shall exhibit a high degree of character & integrity.
4. Only one recipient per college/program will be awarded. In case the college/program will have no nominees/recipients, the awarding ceremony will still commence. Likewise, the GCAAI Board of Trustees can directly, without prejudice to the respective college, nominate and evaluate for such an award though the college/program have already a candidate.
5. Only living candidates are considered. In the event of the death of a candidate between notification of the award and the award ceremony, the award may be given posthumously with the permission of the family and the GCAAI. In the event that the awardee declines to accept the award and/or fails to attend the awarding ceremonies, the GCAAI reserves the right to withdraw the award.
6. A GC graduate sitting as a member of the selection committee or a member of the GCAAI Board of Trustees is not eligible to receive the award. This individual is eligible for nomination after a one-year waiting period from the date he/she step down from his/her role.
7. Recipients are only eligible for one GC-DASA in a lifetime.
8. The recipients will receive a plaque or certificate of recognition and will be posted on the official FB page of the GCAAI. It will announce the annual recipients through various methods and honor each recipient with the appropriate recognition.
9. If for any reason, the GCAAI is not able to conduct the Awards as planned (including due to tampering, unauthorized intervention, fraud, technical difficulties, pandemic, or any other causes beyond the control of the organizer which impact the administration, security, fairness, integrity or proper conduct of the Awards), then they reserve the right in their sole discretion to make any reasonable variation to the Awarding Ceremony.
10. The Nomination Committee and/or GCAAI Board of Trustees may disqualify any nominee that they reasonably determine has breached any of the terms and conditions or otherwise engaged in unlawful or other improper conduct that may bring the Awards into disrepute or jeopardize the fair and proper conduct of the Awards.
11. Other guidelines may be promulgated as decided by the GCAAI Board of Trustees.
## NOMINATION COMMITTEE:
The Nomination Committee (NC) may be organized by the Dean/OIC in every college and/or the GCAAI. The committee must be composed of at least three (3) members from their respective colleges. The total count of committee members should be an odd number. The chairperson of the NC shall be the Dean or its duly authorized representative and for the GCAAI, any member can initiate such nomination. No member of the NC must be related within the second degree of consanguinity or affinity to any of the nominees/candidates for awards. If the Dean/OIC is related within the second degree of consanguinity or affinity to any of the candidates for awards, the Dean/OIC must inhibit him/herself from participating in the process. The approval shall come from the person next in rank.
The NC shall:
1. Abide by the criteria set forth by the GCAAI.
2. For nominees endorsed by the College, it shall issue a certificate of authenticity to all the documents that will be submitted to the GCAAI.
3. Deliberate on the qualifications of the nominees for the awards based on the criteria and the documents submitted.
4. Recommend to the GCAAI Board of Trustees or its Alumni Association President the result of nomination for approval.
5. Recommend to the GCAAI Board of Trustees or its Alumni Association President the resolution on any related issue that may arise from the results of the awards.
6. Ensure that the guidelines stipulated herein are followed.
## NOMINATION AND SELECTION PROCESS:
1. Complete the online/hard copy Nomination Form. This also forms part of the terms and conditions.
2. Nominators will receive an auto-generated copy via email of what they submitted, i.e., the nomination form including any attachments. In any case, it is not available, the NC may determine what means of notification it will conduct to the nominators to confirm the submission of the nomination form and its attachments.
3. Nominees and nominators will be contacted by the NC/GCAAI within two days of receipt of the nomination to acknowledge receipt.
4. A nominee may be asked to clarify or submit further information and evidence to assist the NC/GCAAI in assessing the nomination at any stage of the selection process.
5. The NC/GCAAI may also seek verification of the nominee’s achievements from their current or previous employer or other relevant individual or organization representative. By being nominated for this Award, the nominees are giving consent to the NC/GCAAI to contact these individuals to verify nominations.
6. Nominations will be reviewed and shortlisted by the NC/GCAAI and nominees who have not been shortlisted will be notified. The GC-DASA candidates will be asked to submit a brief profile for this purpose. The candidate shall garner a total score of 60 points to be included in the shortlist. If a nominee cannot meet the required points, he/she is automatically excluded from the shortlist.
7. Every member of the NC will recommend/endorse the final candidate to the GCAAI President and then, the GCAAI Board of Trustees, sitting as Awards Selection Committee, will issue a confirmation through a Board Resolution. The report on the results of the NC shall be signed by all members of the committee and certified by the chairperson.
8. In any case that such protest will be filed during the confirmation of the final candidates, the final decision on the protest shall be decided by the GCAAI Board of Trustees. The GCAAI Board of Trustees’ decision is final on all matters relating to the nomination process and the selection of award recipients, and they are not required to provide feedback as to why a nomination was unsuccessful.
**CRITERIA:**

# GCian Alumni Makes Name on OSLP 2023
[Image: GCian Alumni Makes Name on OSLP 2023]
[Alumni](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/alumni/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, October 11, 2023**
Mr. Marko L. Aratea and Mr. Nieki E. Garigo, both alumni from Gordon College’s College of Education, Arts, and Sciences (CEAS), stand out as finalists during the award ceremony for Outstanding Student Leaders of the Philippines (OSLP) 2023 held at Teacher’s Camp, Baguio City, on October 1, 2023.
In an interview with The Forefront, Aratea and Garigo claimed that the Office of Student Welfare and Service (OSWS) and GC-CEAS had announced that they would represent the institution in the aforementioned competition and directed all requirements to Transforming Advocates and Champions (TRAC).
“I was astonished and exhilarated when it was announced that I was one of the top 20 finalists. Knowing that out of 135 applicants across our nation and at various colleges and universities, I was chosen. For me, I consider that one of the great achievements that I had,” Aratea said, reflecting on how he felt when he knew that he was one of the finalists.
While Garigo stated, “Syempre napasigaw ako that time, because bago pa dumating sa point na yan ay marami kaming dinaanang proseso at screening. I still remember the day where they conducted our final interview and sakto pang nakaduty ako sa Cooperating School ko for my practice teaching, and as in, medyo mahina ang signal niyan kaya during my 1-hour interview, ay naroroon ako sa ilalim ng manga ng Regional Science High School para maging stable ang internet, and sobrang naging successful naman ng interview dahil every time na may mga ganyang screening, kasi ako I always put in my mind na parang “kachikahan” ko lang ang interviewer at sobrang ganda ng discussion namin, especially yung point na may SDG pa sa question, like I am not ready that time dahil ang nasa isip ko talaga niyan ang lesson sa susunod na subject na tuturuan ko. But thank God because everything went well.”
They expressed their deepest gratitude to TRAC for the opportunity they attained and to their co-finalists from different schools. The representatives would also like to thank Gordon College, the GC Alumni Association, Inc., GC-RDCES, GC-OSWS, GC-CEAS, their families, and lastly, the Almighty God.
“Be who you are, be true to yourself, and always know your advocacy by heart and mind. Think of this competition as a way for you to raise your voice as a student leader, an advocate, and a champion,” Aratea said, while Garigo stated, “Be a leader na hindi tuta; be a leader na boses ng mag-aaral; be a leader who is pro-people, pro-student, and pro-community,” as their advice for those aspiring student leaders of the institution.
Aratea was also hailed as the champion for the Student Research Paper Presentation at the said event.

# PESO-TCLASS Visits Gordon College for BSN Program Benchmarking and COPC Preparation
[Image: PESO-TCLASS Visits Gordon College for BSN Program Benchmarking and COPC Preparation]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Friday, August 29, 2025**
Gordon College warmly welcomed the Public Employment Service Office – Tarlac Center for Learning and Skills Success (PESO-TCLASS) under the Provincial Government of Tarlac last August 26, 2025, for a benchmarking visit.
As part of their preparations to offer the Bachelor of Science in Nursing (BSN) program and apply for a Certificate of Program Compliance (COPC), the PESO-TCLASS delegation, headed by Dr. Maria Lira Reyes – PESO Manager and School Administrator, engaged with Gordon College to learn from our best practices in curriculum development, faculty qualifications, library and physical facilities, equipment, support services, and quality assurance systems. The visit provided them with valuable insights and guidance on meeting the minimum standards required for program compliance.
This knowledge-sharing initiative reflects Gordon College’s continued commitment to collaboration and capacity-building among academic institutions.

[Image: GC-SSC benchmarks student leadership and governance among HEIs]

**Wednesday, November 19, 2025**
In line with the commitment of the Student Council in upholding the inherent rights and cardinal necessities of the academic community, the 𝗚𝗼𝗿𝗱𝗼𝗻 𝗖𝗼𝗹𝗹𝗲𝗴𝗲 – 𝗦𝘂𝗽𝗿𝗲𝗺𝗲 𝗦𝘁𝘂𝗱𝗲𝗻𝘁 𝗖𝗼𝘂𝗻𝗰𝗶𝗹 (GC-SSC) conducts a benchmarking activity on best practices, strategies for student leadership development, and organizational dynamics on November 17-18, 2025, at 𝘾𝙚𝙣𝙩𝙧𝙖𝙡 𝙇𝙪𝙯𝙤𝙣 𝙎𝙩𝙖𝙩𝙚 𝙐𝙣𝙞𝙫𝙚𝙧𝙨𝙞𝙩𝙮, 𝙏𝙖𝙧𝙡𝙖𝙘 𝙎𝙩𝙖𝙩𝙚 𝙐𝙣𝙞𝙫𝙚𝙧𝙨𝙞𝙩𝙮, 𝙖𝙣𝙙 𝘽𝙪𝙡𝙖𝙘𝙖𝙣 𝙎𝙩𝙖𝙩𝙚 𝙐𝙣𝙞𝙫𝙚𝙧𝙨𝙞𝙩𝙮. # Gordon College Joins the 2025 Times Higher Education Impact Rankings
[Image: Gordon College Joins the 2025 Times Higher Education Impact Rankings]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, June 18, 2025**
We are proud to announce that Gordon College has officially earned a place in the prestigious Times Higher Education (THE) Impact Rankings 2025! Despite being a newcomer to the global stage, GC was ranked among the top 1,500+ out of 2,526 higher education institutions (HEIs) from 130 countries, assessed for their contributions to achieving the United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (UN SDGs)—a recognition based on universities’ social impact through research, stewardship, outreach, and teaching.
Gordon College participated in four SDG categories and earned the following standings:
• SDG 3 – Good Health and Well-being: Ranked 1001–1500
• SDG 4 – Quality Education: Ranked 1501+
• SDG 10 – Reduced Inequalities: Ranked 1001+
• SDG 17 – Partnerships for the Goals: Ranked 1501+
More than just recognition, this milestone reflects our evolving vision—to become a globally recognized local institution making meaningful contributions to global progress through inclusive, equitable, and sustainable development in higher education.

# GC holds mental health seminar, boosts self-harm awareness among students
[Image: GC holds mental health seminar, boosts self-harm awareness among students]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Monday, March 23, 2026**
To raise awareness on self-harm, the Gordon College – Office of Student Welfare and Services (GC-OSWS), in collaboration with GC – Guidance Counseling Unit (GC-GCU), conducted the “Nature of Self-Harm: Awareness, Compassion, and Prevention” seminar last March 13 at the Function Hall, GC – Main Building.
Graced by Resource Speaker Erin RiegodeDios Asio, a Master of Arts in Education Major in Behavioral Sciences graduate, the seminar featured how Non-Suicidal Self-Injury (NSSI), also known as self-harm, is different from suicide.
According to Asio, self-harm is a way of coping intense feelings by hurting oneself on purpose. The intention of doing self-harm is to feel alive and escape emotional pain.
Additionally, she stated that self-harm used to be listed as a symptom of Borderline Personality Disorder (BPD), a pervasive disorder that affects the whole personality. Most people who self-harm do not have BPD, but people who have BPD may have these tendencies.
The seminar went further and discussed the different types of self-harm: cutting, burning, and hitting. If NSSI is done occasionally, there is a risk of making an unintentional mistake that could lead to accidental death.
Asio explained the phases of taking action when someone is afflicted with self-harm. Where the first phase is listening to someone when they open up about hurting themselves. Then, offer support toward counseling.
Then followed by the causes, myths, and how to cope with self-harm in a healthy way. She also told students that they should check up on each other afterwards.
The keynote speaker also underscored the importance of promoting a stigma-free, healthy, and supportive environment within the school.
Students are welcome at the GCU office for free counseling services to those struggling with their mental health. | via Jasmine Zeah Llagas/The FOREFRONT
Photos by Lheeon Owen Dela Rosa and Marian Sualibio

### Source: https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/%f0%9d%90%8c%f0%9d%90%80%f0%9d%90%98%f0%9d%90%80%f0%9d%90%91%f0%9d%90%88-%f0%9d%90%91%f0%9d%90%a2%f0%9d%90%ac%f0%9d%90%9e%f0%9d%90%ac-%f0%9d%90%86%f0%9d%90%82-%f0%9d%90%a5%f0%9d%90%9a%f0%9d%90%ae

# 𝐌𝐀𝐘𝐀𝐑𝐈 𝐑𝐢𝐬𝐞𝐬: 𝐆𝐂 𝐥𝐚𝐮𝐧𝐜𝐡𝐞𝐬 𝐟𝐢𝐫𝐬𝐭 𝐋𝐆𝐁𝐓𝐐𝐈𝐀+ 𝐒𝐭𝐮𝐝𝐞𝐧𝐭 𝐎𝐫𝐠𝐚𝐧𝐢𝐳𝐚𝐭𝐢𝐨𝐧
[Image: 𝐌𝐀𝐘𝐀𝐑𝐈 𝐑𝐢𝐬𝐞𝐬: 𝐆𝐂 𝐥𝐚𝐮𝐧𝐜𝐡𝐞𝐬 𝐟𝐢𝐫𝐬𝐭 𝐋𝐆𝐁𝐓𝐐𝐈𝐀+ 𝐒𝐭𝐮𝐝𝐞𝐧𝐭 𝐎𝐫𝐠𝐚𝐧𝐢𝐳𝐚𝐭𝐢𝐨𝐧]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, June 18, 2025**
Gordon College established its first LGBTQIA+ Student Organization named “Mga Anak ng Yugtong Apo para sa Respeto at Inklusyon (MAYARI),” in line with the celebration of Pride Month this June.
MAYARI aims to champion the rights and well-being of LGBTQIA+ students within the college and beyond.
Furthermore, Mr. Marko Aratea, organization adviser of MAYARI, confirmed that the organization has developed rapidly and has profound objectives.
“We, at MAYARI, are currently in the process of becoming a formally recognized civic organization at Gordon College, but we will get there very soon!” Mr. Aratea stated.
Moreover, MAYARI was formed with both students and the Office of Student Welfare and Services (OSWS) in mind as a dedicated community for LGBTQIA+ students and their allies. Both Mr. Aratea and co-adviser Mr. Christian Loid Valenzuela started as the founding members who led the creation of the constitution, set clear objectives, and met institutional standards.
The central aim of MAYARI is to establish a safe and inclusive space for LGBTQIA+ students. Mr. Aratea outlined the organization’s primary objectives: “MAYARI aims to provide a safe and inclusive space for LGBTQIA+ students and allies in our humble institution. Our main goals are to promote visibility, inclusivity, respect, empowerment, and solidarity.”
MAYARI intends to accomplish these objectives through education by providing LGBTQIA+ seminars, webinars, gender sensitivity programs, and opportunities for creativity and leadership.
Furthermore, one of the important goals of MAYARI is to handle issues affecting LGBTQIA+ individuals, mainly discrimination, mental health challenges, and lack of community understanding.
“MAYARI is a civic organization for LGBTQIA+ and allies in GC, also serving as a platform to address catalysts related to gender-based concerns through formal dialogues programs and activities that will equip relevant stakeholders regarding said concerns and practical solutions,” MAYARI’s adviser affirmed.
Meanwhile, the organization will implement peer support initiatives, explore partnerships with the college’s Diversity, Equity, and Inclusion (DEI) office, and launch extensive awareness campaigns on SOGIESC (Sexual Orientation, Gender Identity, Gender Expression, and Sex Characteristics).
Looking beyond symbolic gestures, MAYARI envisions a future where “total acceptance” is deeply ingrained in the institution’s policies and culture.
“Total acceptance means more than symbolic gestures—it involves policy and culture change,” Mr. Aratea emphasized and further discussed that the organization aims to integrate inclusivity into the college’s systemic operations rather than limiting it to mere statements.

# PRC On-site Caravan 2025 Lands with a Bang
[Image: PRC On-site Caravan 2025 Lands with a Bang]
[Alumni](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/alumni/) [CCS](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/ccs/) [IPDEA](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/ipdea/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Friday, May 9, 2025**
𝐏𝐑𝐎𝐅𝐄𝐒𝐒𝐈𝐎𝐍𝐀𝐋 𝐑𝐄𝐆𝐔𝐋𝐀𝐓𝐈𝐎𝐍 𝐂𝐎𝐌𝐌𝐈𝐒𝐒𝐈𝐎𝐍 (𝐏𝐑𝐂) 𝐎𝐍-𝐒𝐈𝐓𝐄 𝐒𝐄𝐑𝐕𝐈𝐂𝐄 𝐂𝐀𝐑𝐀𝐕𝐀𝐍
GORDON COLLEGE | OLONGAPO CITY, ZAMBALES
May 9, 2025
[Image: ✨]
It’s a wrap!
[Image: ✨]
The PRC On-Site Service Caravan at Gordon College was a resounding success, delivering a total of 549 services to Professionals in a single day. This initiative significantly improved access to essential PRC services within the local community—and is part of our ongoing commitment to support not only our Alumni but also to all Professionals.
Breakdown of Services Rendered:
• Renewal – 138
• Initial Registration – 60
• Duplicate – 11
• Certification – 170
• Authentication – 162
• Certificate of Special Order (COS) – 8
TOTAL – 549
A special acknowledgment to the dedicated PRC Committee for their exemplary service and leadership:
Ms. Elaine Perez Espela, Ms. Glenda B. Sapon (Team Leader), Ms. Joana Benigna Pabelonia, Mr. Joselito Dulay, and Mr. Angelo Pedro Hernandez.
We are also grateful to the student organizations for their invaluable support and active participation:
• College of Computer Studies Student Council (CCS SC)
• GCCCS Empowered League of Information Technology Education Students (ELITES)
Sincere appreciation goes to the VP for Institutional Planning, Development, and External Affairs, Dr. Kristin Joy A. Mendoza, for her instrumental role in the successful implementation of this initiative.
We would also like to express our heartfelt thanks to Mr. Joseph Angelo B. Pusing GCAAI President and Ms. Denise Lou Punzalan for spearheading this event, and to the Board of Trustees and Working Committee of the Gordon College Alumni Association Inc. (GCAAI) for their full support and commitment in making this initiative a success.
Special thanks to Dr. Imelda DP Soriano, College President, for her unwavering support in bringing services closer to the community.
This caravan is not just a service initiative—it’s our way of helping empower our alumni community as they continue their professional journeys.
Thank you to everyone who contributed to the success of the PRC On-Site Service Caravan. Together, we continue to broaden access to professional services and empower our community.

# Gordon College receives Academic Excellence Award 2025
[Image: Gordon College receives Academic Excellence Award 2025]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Tuesday, December 9, 2025**
Gordon College receives the prestigious Academic Excellence Award from ALCUCOA for significantly demonstrating excellence in education through its outstanding performance in the recent Licensure Examinations conducted by the Professional Regulation Commission (PRC).
The award specifically recognizes the college’s exceptional results, which include:
1. November 2025 Midwifery Licensure Examination: Achieving a 100% passing rate for first-time takers, far exceeding the national average. This perfect score solidifies Gordon College’s reputation for producing highly competent health professionals.
2. May 2025 Philippine Nurses Licensure Examination (PNLE): Gordon College earns national recognition for producing top-ranking examinees.
Top 9 Topnotchers: Martin Jorge Bernal Alcantara and Loren Mirador Mistica
3. May 2025 Philippine Nurses Licensure Examination (PNLE): Also producing a top-ranking examinee:
Top 8 Topnotcher: Rose Sheilla Mae D. Domingo
This remarkable achievement in both the Midwifery and Nursing licensure exams underscores the effectiveness of Gordon College’s rigorous academic programs, the dedication of its faculty, and the quality of its students.
With this significant recognition, the college is reinvigorated and eager to pursue its commitment to providing quality higher education and continuing its mission to produce competent, industry-ready alumni who are well-equipped to excel in their respective fields.

# GC ALCUCOA Accreditation 2025
[Image: GC ALCUCOA Accreditation 2025]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, November 26, 2025**
Nagsimula na ang tatlong-araw na akreditasyon ng Association of Local Colleges and Universities Commission on Accreditation (ALCUCOA) sa Gordon College.
Ngayong taon, ang Bachelor of Science in Business Administration (BSBA) Major in Marketing Management ay nangangarap makamit ang Level III Accreditation.
Samantala, ang Bachelor of Science in Accountancy (BSA), Bachelor in Elementary Education (BEEd), Bachelor of Early Childhood Education (BECEd), Bachelor of Secondary Education Major in Filipino (BSEd-Fil), at Bachelor of Secondary Education Major in Science (BSEd-Sci) ay target naman ang Level II Accreditation.
Bukod dito, ang Bachelor of Culture and Arts Education (BCAEd) at Bachelor of Physical Education (BPEd) ay nominado bilang mga kandidato para sa akreditasyon ngayong taon.
Suportado ito ni Mayor [Atty. Rolen Paulino Jr.](https://www.facebook.com/mayorlenjpaulino?__cft__[0]=AZU3QvTqhQlkmJYMC3O-Ag6rBVcz3QYgae7wuVrFTGSN7kZ89KmJdl5ubPj8hN-KEPPY6EcfTQTGxYHZRibHJrQGqb402VnV70UrI9lSc8vH5vinQtVkACLAtBxyIbEXsw18OHsjlIkfBfyeqEXf7TUole-b6rZBCRPWdXyT96ESDoVWz3Fkm2GAvU73XCQkG6peAqxX1fWecL7WvNOwujSLGUFmxQTez5zZwVPmWci_DA&__tn__=-]K-y-R), na patuloy na tumatayong kaagapay ng Gordon College sa pagtataguyod ng kalidad na edukasyon para sa bawat Olongapeño.
Maraming salamat sa lahat ng accreditors; Library
Prof. Rolingel Calilung, BPED Prof. Wendell Delos Reyes, BCAED Dr. Priscilla Minas, BSA Prof. Anselmo Giron, BEED Dr. Elizabeth Montero, BECED Dr. Carolina Duka, BSED Fil Dr. Ronald Gonzales, BSED Sci Dr. Michelle Ventura, BSBA MKT Dr. Daniel Hebron, Supervising Accreditor Dr. Montero, Supervising TA – Dr. Alni Gayjahmin Casacop, at TA – Prof. Jezreel Hidalgo.
Gaganapin ang Accreditation 2025 mula Nobyembre 26–28, 2025 sa Function Hall, Gordon College Main Building.  
CTTO: **[Olongapo City Information Center](https://www.facebook.com/olongapopublicaffairs/posts/pfbid0YFaUWbtKVdzrpADpi4ujGyic95pLkB8jYFXU2fXqRUiNJGgV2sYNpaN6NpmbMUpl?__cft__[0]=AZU3QvTqhQlkmJYMC3O-Ag6rBVcz3QYgae7wuVrFTGSN7kZ89KmJdl5ubPj8hN-KEPPY6EcfTQTGxYHZRibHJrQGqb402VnV70UrI9lSc8vH5vinQtVkACLAtBxyIbEXsw18OHsjlIkfBfyeqEXf7TUole-b6rZBCRPWdXyT96ESDoVWz3Fkm2GAvU73XCQkG6peAqxX1fWecL7WvNOwujSLGUFmxQTez5zZwVPmWci_DA&__tn__=%2CO%2CP-y-R)**

# Implementation of the School Uniform Policy
[Image: Implementation of the School Uniform Policy]
**Wednesday, July 30, 2025**
𝐀𝐃𝐕𝐈𝐒𝐎𝐑𝐘
The implementation of the school uniform policy will begin on 𝐒𝐞𝐩𝐭𝐞𝐦𝐛𝐞𝐫 𝟖, 𝟐𝟎𝟐𝟓, 𝐟𝐨𝐫 𝐅𝐫𝐞𝐬𝐡𝐦𝐞𝐧 𝐚𝐧𝐝 𝐓𝐫𝐚𝐧𝐬𝐟𝐞𝐫𝐞𝐞𝐬, while 𝐒𝐨𝐩𝐡𝐨𝐦𝐨𝐫𝐞𝐬 𝐭𝐨 𝐒𝐞𝐧𝐢𝐨𝐫𝐬 𝐰𝐢𝐥𝐥 𝐛𝐞 𝐫𝐞𝐪𝐮𝐢𝐫𝐞𝐝 𝐭𝐨 𝐰𝐞𝐚𝐫 𝐭𝐡𝐞𝐢𝐫 𝐬𝐜𝐡𝐨𝐨𝐥 𝐮𝐧𝐢𝐟𝐨𝐫𝐦𝐬 𝐬𝐭𝐚𝐫𝐭𝐢𝐧𝐠 𝐀𝐮𝐠𝐮𝐬𝐭 𝟏𝟖, 𝟐𝟎𝟐𝟓.
𝐖𝐨𝐫𝐤𝐢𝐧𝐠 𝐒𝐭𝐮𝐝𝐞𝐧𝐭𝐬 must accomplish their Uniform Exemption Pass from 𝐀𝐮𝐠𝐮𝐬𝐭 𝟏 𝐭𝐨 𝟏𝟓, 𝟐𝟎𝟐𝟓 𝐚𝐭 𝐫𝐨𝐨𝐦 𝟏𝟐𝟐 at the office of our Student Discipline Officers.
The following shall 𝐧𝐨𝐭 𝐛𝐞 𝐚𝐥𝐥𝐨𝐰𝐞𝐝:
1. Sleeveless tops, spaghetti straps, razor backs, tubes, hanging or mid rib blouse
2. All types of shorts (walking shorts, cycling shorts, tokong) tight fitting (leggings) and torn or mutilated jeans
3. Clothes with frayed hems
4. Blouses and dresses with plunging necklines/backless
5. Garments that are backless and See-through attires
6. Miniskirts (more than 2 inches above the knee) except where curriculum requires.
7. Long Skirts with high slit
8. Jogging pants (allowed only for PE schedule)
9. Slippers (exemptions: medical reason, weather conditions)
10. T-shirts with vulgar, indecent, suggestive points, or anything which advertise or promote the use of drugs, alcohol, or tobacco.
11. Clogs (e.g., Crocs) may be worn if for medical reasons and during inclement weather (heavy rains and excessive heat).

# Final presentation of Project METRO
[Image: Final presentation of Project METRO]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Friday, April 17, 2026**
April 16, 2026 | Final presentation of Project METRO: Monitoring Enforcement Tracking Resource Online with DTI Zambales and Gordon College – College of Computer Studies.
Present during the final presentation are DTI3 CPD Acting Division Chief Jose Antonio Toledo, DTI Zambales CPD Division Chief Neil John M. Fabay, Gordon College CCS Dean Ronnie D. Luy, Gordon College CCS Asst Dean Armilyn T. Martinez, and Gordon College CCS Prof. Reynaldo G. Bautista. The panelist provided expert guidance and valuable insights to further refining the system.
Project METRO is intended to make market inspections and fair trade monitoring more efficient, transparent, and fully digitized. In practical terms, the system aims to help government personnel carry out inspections more systematically while improving documentation, reporting, and coordination in the field.
The use of a digital platform for inspections will also help strengthen public trust by making enforcement more transparent and easier to document. In many cases, digitized systems can support more consistent monitoring while reducing gaps in manual processes.

# Online Application for GC Admission Test Procedure (1st Semester, A.Y. 2024-2025)
[Image: Online Application for GC Admission Test Procedure (1st Semester, A.Y. 2024-2025)]
[OSWS](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/osws/)
**Monday, December 18, 2023**
**[Image: 🟢]
STEP 1. CREATE ONLINE PORTAL ACCOUNT**
1.1 Go to Gordon College Admission Portal login page (<https://gordoncollegeccs.edu.ph/gca>)
1.2 Click “Create Account”
1.3 Type your email address, given name, last name, date of birth, contact number, desired password and select type of student (New Student, Transferee, Second Courser)
1.4 Click “Register”
Notes:
o A valid and existing email address is required in this process.
o Do not create multiple accounts as this will be a ground for disapproval of application.
· Do not forget your email and password
**[Image: 🟢]
STEP 2. LOG ON TO YOUR ACCOUNT**
2.1 Go to the portal login page
2.2 Type your previously registered email address and password then click “Log In”
2.3 You will be directed to the portal Dashboard.
2.4 The Dashboard displays the status of your application.
[Image: 🟢]
 **STEP 3. PROFILING**
3.1 On the upper left side of the page, click “Upload Photo” to upload your recent photo.
Guide:
o 2×2 colored picture, white background
o Formal pose wearing collared top, No eyeglasses or any accessories like earrings.
o Picture should be taken not later than one week prior to filing of application.
3.2 On the Menu, click “Profile”
3.3 Completely and accurately fill out the forms (Personal Information, Family Background and Desired Programs)
3.4 Read the Gordon College General Data Privacy Notice and click the CHECKBOX.
3.5 Review the form and click “Submit”
**[Image: 🟢]
STEP 4. UPLOADING OF DOCUMENTS**
4.1 On the Menu, click “Uploads”
4.2 Upload scanned or clear image of the required documents. Click the placeholder to browse for an image to upload.
4.3 The image file should be less than 2MB to avoid uploading errors
Required documents:
o Recent Good Moral Character Certificate
o PSA Authenticated Birth Certificate
o Senior High School ID or Any Valid ID Card
– Click “Submit” to finalize the uploading.
[Image: 🟢]
 **STEP 5. GCAT Schedule**
5.1 The schedule of GCAT will be posted to your portal account within three (3) working days after submission of COMPLETE application and required documents.
5.2 Upon posting of the test schedule on the online portal, you may download and print the following:
o GCAT Schedule slip
o Student Information Sheet
————————————————–
What to bring on the test day?
1. GCAT Schedule Slip
2. Student Information Sheets
3. Senior High School or any valid ID Card
5. Pencil and Pen
Note: Please be informed that all applications shall be submitted online to prevent the spread of Covid-19.

# New Supreme Student Council proclaimed

[OSWS](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/osws/) [SSC](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/ssc/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Tuesday, May 6, 2025**
FRONTLINES | 𝑰𝒏 𝒕𝒉𝒆 𝒄𝒂𝒍𝒍 𝒕𝒐 𝒕𝒉𝒊𝒏𝒌 𝒘𝒊𝒔𝒆 𝒂𝒏𝒅 𝒗𝒐𝒕𝒆 𝒔𝒎𝒂𝒓𝒕, 𝒄𝒂𝒏 𝒂𝒏𝒚𝒐𝒏𝒆 𝒓𝒆𝒂𝒍𝒍𝒚 𝒔𝒂𝒚, ‘𝑰 𝒕𝒉𝒐𝒖𝒈𝒉𝒕 𝒘𝒊𝒔𝒆𝒍𝒚 𝒂𝒏𝒅 𝒗𝒐𝒕𝒆𝒅 𝒔𝒎𝒂𝒓𝒕’?

𝐂𝐡𝐚𝐢𝐫𝐦𝐚𝐧: 𝐒𝐚𝐦𝐨𝐧𝐭𝐞, 𝐉𝐮𝐥𝐢𝐞𝐫
𝐕𝐢𝐜𝐞 𝐂𝐡𝐚𝐢𝐫𝐦𝐚𝐧: 𝐏𝐫𝐚𝐝𝐚𝐬, 𝐃𝐡𝐚𝐧𝐚 𝐃𝐞𝐥

# TF SECURES LHEPC SPOT
[Image: TF SECURES LHEPC SPOT]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Saturday, February 14, 2026**
The Forefront journos once again demonstrated their grit and skills in campus journalism, securing several awards at the 24th Regional Higher Education Press Conference (RHEPC), held at Palm Cabanas Resort, Arayat, Pampanga, from February 11 to 13.
Two of the publication’s outgoing executive members, John Patrick Mateo (Editor-in-Chief) and Lance Isaac Leon (Associate Editor), were recognized as Outstanding Tertiary Campus Journalists (OTCJ) in Region III.
In the group category, the pub’s entry for Opening Billboard ranked 4th Place overall.
In individual categories, delegates from the publication bagged nine (9) awards as listed below.
Angelique Jose
1st Place – Traditional Literary Graphics Illustration (English)
Lance Isaac Leon
2nd Place – Editorial Writing (English)
3rd Place – Opinion Writing (English)
John Patrick Mateo
3rd Place – Devcom Writing (Filipino)
John Christian Rocero
3rd Place – Photojournalism (English)
Mary Isobelle Arcilla
5th Place – Traditional Comic Strip Drawing (English)
6th Place – Traditional Editorial Cartooning (English)
Keane Rafael Abdon
8th Place – News Writing (English)
Mikhaela Sagun
10th Place – Poetry Writing (English)
Overall, The Forefront is the 11th Highest Pointer in individual categories against 42 participating college publications in Central Luzon.
Additionally, Keane Rafael Abdon is the newly elected division coordinator of Olongapo City for the Young Journalist Association of Region III (YJAR-III).
Five campus journalists from the publication, who garnered top 5 placements in their respective categories, will advance to the 21st Luzon-Wide Higher Education Press Conference (LHEPC) in Tuguegarao City, Cagayan, next month to compete in eight categories. | via Mikhaela Sagun and Lance Isaac Leon, THE FOREFRONT
Photos by Guiller T. Marila and John Christian J. Rocero

# 𝐏𝐚𝐠𝐥𝐢𝐥𝐢𝐩𝐚𝐭 𝐧𝐠 𝐤𝐚𝐩𝐚𝐧𝐠𝐲𝐚𝐫𝐢𝐡𝐚𝐧 𝐬𝐚 𝐛𝐚𝐠𝐨𝐧𝐠 𝐭𝐞𝐫𝐦𝐢𝐧𝐨, 𝐩𝐨𝐫𝐦𝐚𝐥 𝐧𝐚𝐧𝐠 𝐧𝐚𝐢𝐬𝐚𝐤𝐚𝐭𝐮𝐩𝐚𝐫𝐚𝐧
[Image: 𝐏𝐚𝐠𝐥𝐢𝐥𝐢𝐩𝐚𝐭 𝐧𝐠 𝐤𝐚𝐩𝐚𝐧𝐠𝐲𝐚𝐫𝐢𝐡𝐚𝐧 𝐬𝐚 𝐛𝐚𝐠𝐨𝐧𝐠 𝐭𝐞𝐫𝐦𝐢𝐧𝐨, 𝐩𝐨𝐫𝐦𝐚𝐥 𝐧𝐚𝐧𝐠 𝐧𝐚𝐢𝐬𝐚𝐤𝐚𝐭𝐮𝐩𝐚𝐫𝐚𝐧]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Friday, July 18, 2025**
GORDON COLLEGE — Isinalin na ang mga posisyon at kapangyarihan sa mga bagong opisyales, gayundin ang pagpapakilala sa mga bagong kakatawan sa mga GCians sa katatapos na Supreme Student Council Turnover Ceremony na ginanap sa PE Hall nitong ika-14 ng Hulyo.
Nasaksihan din ng mga lider-estudyante mula sa iba’t ibang organisasyon sa loob ng institusyon ang nabanggit na seremonya.
Bilang panimula, binigyang diin ng noo’y SSC Chairperson Frances Daphne Mobo ang seremonya sa paglalahad ng Budget at Accomplishment Reports ng kanyang termino.
Samantala, inamin ni Mobo na nabigo nilang tuparin ang ilang mga pangakong proyekto para sa mga mag-aaral.
“Failures make us better individuals. Leadership teaches you how to be accountable [for] your own actions and how to accept constructive criticism. To our student leaders present here today, do remember that leadership is not about positions or titles because those are temporary. Leadership is about passion and dedication,” aniya.
Bukod dito, binigyan ng pagkakataon ang bagong termino upang talakayin at bigyang linaw ang kanilang mga inihandang proyekto at aktibidades sa harap ng kanilang mga kapwa lider-estudyante.
Sumatutal, mayroong 12 na proyekto at 17 na aktibidades na gustong ilunsad ang naturang termino sa pangunguna ni ngayo’y SSC Chairperson Julier Samonte. Ilan dito ay ang GCLAMP 2.0: Enhancing Access in the GCLAMP, CODE 200: Tech Industry Partnership Found, BIN-novation: Classroom for a Cleaner Future para sa kanilang proyekto, samantala ay inahain naman ang Student First Starter Packs: College Life Unboxed, Think Green: Green Revolutions for GCians, Beyond the Myths: Understanding HIV/AIDS & Prevention para sa kanilang mga aktibidades.
Ngunit ang lahat ng kanilang programa ay pawang mga panukala pa lamang at posibleng mayroong ibang hindi mapayagang mailunsad, paglilinaw ng bagong konseho.
Bilang karagdagan sa mga pangako ni Samonte, bagong kinatawan ng konseho, nais niya at ng kaniyang konseho na palawakin ang komunikasyon at transparency sa pagsasagawa ng regular student consultations, open forums, at online suggestion platforms para marinig ang tunay na saloobin ng mga estudyante. Kasama rin dito ang paglabas ng general plan of actions, budget breakdowns, at accomplishment reports upang mas mapanatili ang tiwala ng bawat estudyante sa konseho.
“Pangatlo, mas makabuluhang events para sa lahat. Gagawin nating inclusive at meaningful ang bawat SSC-led event. Hindi lang ito basta kasiyahan dahil gagawa rin po tayo ng mga aktibidad na magtataguyod ng leadership, advocacy, at student development.”
“Pang apat, pagpapalakas ng bawat student organization sa bawat departamento. Bilang inyong chairperson, sisiguraduhin kong makakaramdam ng buong suporta ang bawat departamental organization,” dagdag pa niya.
Paalala naman ng bagong SSC Chairperson na ang kaniyang pagkapanalo noong nakaraang eleksyon ay hindi lamang tagumpay ng isang tao kundi tagumpay rin ng bawat estudyante na naniniwala na pwedeng marinig, makilahok, at maging bahagi ng pagbabago.
\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_
Ang The Forefront, opisyal na publikasyon ng Dalubhasaan, ay mananatiling nakamasid sa mga isasakatuparang programa’t proyekto ng Supreme Student Council na magpapatunay na [#NoStudentLeftBehind](https://www.facebook.com/hashtag/nostudentleftbehind?__eep__=6&__cft__[0]=AZUkpyVY36DTjuBX_LBfWl1h5OP6SP8zSgH13ZOCR57qpBG2lvcnA_I0eP3OwXk_sxf7aRgZa69lsUJjmMQHbHu8xs2v1a0HnCWW9WXGSwEHAGPaerl4e8IKYXgllMeCrIKjYNpwvOkmG3U8rOsk_wNIfahDcxsTgloutQi60oQnSFHiFr3KfpSyDtumfUmdLUc&__tn__=*NK-R).

# Website Policy
### Website Committee
Gordon College (GC) Website Committee was formally established by Interoffice Memorandum No. 38 Re: Website Committee. The Interoffice Memorandum states the following; composition, duties, and responsibilities of Website Committee, Guidelines in Website Content Development and Management, and Procedure for Posting/Uploading Web Content.
### Composition
GC Website Committee will be composed of a Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson, Website Administrator, and Members.  
For the members, it will be the Head of the Information and Communication Services Unit (ICSU), one (1) faculty member from each department designated by the College Dean, and one (1) staff from each office/unit assigned by the Unit Head.
### Duties and Responsibilities of Website Committee
Based on Interoffice Memo No. 38, the following are the duties and responsibilities of the committee:
1. Develop and recommend web related policies and standards
2. Oversee the design and underlying technology
3. Ensure the website stays current with new technology/trends
4. Implement regular data collection methods
5. Use data collected to guide the framework and infrastructure of the college website
6. Create guiding principles to steer the framework and infrastructure decisions of the college website
7. Work with the Management Information System Unit (MISU) to set timelines for web engine upgrade and new website deployments.
8. Regularly assess the design of the website and make recommendations for improvement
9. Serve as the liaison between the College President and the different department regards content
10. Identify and advocate for web needs and services for the college community.
### Guidelines in Website Content Development and Management
1. Interoffice Memo No. 38, clearly states the guidelines to be observed by the committee in terms of the contents for the website.
2. All news, updates and all other content to be posted on the official website should have clearance from concerned departments/ units and approval from the College President.
3. The Website Committee is tasked to manage the postings on the official website in coordination with the Information and Communication Services Unit (ICSU). The committee shall formulate posting guidelines and ensure proper dissemination.
4. Queries, concerns or issues aired through the official website must be communicated to concerned departments through ICSU who shall also responsible for posting official responses to such queries, concerns or issues whether through online or offline as the situation warrants.
### Procedure for Posting/Uploading Web Content
The following are the guidelines stated in Interoffice Memo No. 38 in terms of posting/uploading content to the college website:
1. Requesting department should submit a copy of the post to ICSU for approval of the College President.
2. After approval, ICSU shall transmit the content to the Website Committee Chairperson and Web Administrator.
### Website Content Submission
The GC Website Committee is pleased to offer the opportunity to share knowledge and expertise by submitting content to the college website with the following guidelines:
* **Guiding Principles**  
  Gordon College is one of the institutions that promote excellent education in Region III and the country. As such, the committee would like to publish and post content, materials, and ideas that support the vision, mission, and goals of the college. The committee also encourages each individual from different colleges and departments to share their experience and insights as one of the people of Gordon College.
* **Style**  
  The Website committee encourages each and everyone to use the current best practices for web writing. These includes:
  + Begin with the conclusion by focusing on the main message and be clear on how you want the reader to feel and respond.
  + Add specific details, facts, figures, quotes, etc.
  + Submit at least one (1) graphs, graphics, or photos that are copyright free with a size of;
    - 1280 pixels by 720 pixels for Hero Banners (Congratulatory Posters)
    - 1200 pixels by 630 pixels for News, Announcement and Notice
    - 250 pixels by 250 pixels for Website Banner
    - 250 pixels by 100 pixels for Logo (Rectangle)
    - 100 pixels by 100 pixels for Logo (Square)
  + Graphs, graphics or photos should be in .jpeg, .png or .svg format
  + Use subheadings that reflect the content and captures the interests of readers
  + Use listed points with ten (10) words or less per item
  + Search the web for other pro tips on best practices
* **Publishing**  
  The committee does not guarantee that it will post your submissions automatically. All published contents shall be well-guided and must receive the approval of concerned departments, as stated on Interoffice Memorandum No. 38.It does guarantee, however, that the article will be read and explore whether or not it is a good fit for the college and the readership. In reviewing submissions, the committee is looking for content that is necessary and relevant to the needs of the stakeholders, especially with the faculty and students. Articles reflecting the inspiring, powerful, responsible, productive, and connected nature of the academe are the ones that we are looking for.
* **Copyright**  
  The committee will only post copyright-free materials submitted by the author and will not pay for articles posted on the website. The college reserves the right to use the submitted article or any part of it depending on the committee’s discretion.
* **Response Time**  
  The goal of the committee is to respond to the requestor as quickly as possible. Depending on the situation, the committee will do its best to respond to each submission in 1 to 3 days.
* **Requestor**  
  The following groups and/or individuals are allowed to submit content for posting:
  + Department Heads
  + Vice Presidents
  + Faculty Members and
  + Administrative Staff
  + Recognized Student Organizations
  + Alumni
### Specific Guidelines
To achieve uniformity and establish the visual identity of the college kindly follow the following in submitting your content;
* **Title and Author Information**  
  All submission must identify (to be written in the body of your message);
  + Topic
  + Title
  + Author’s Full name
  + Author’s Phone Number\*
  + Author’s Email Address\*
  + Department (if applicable)  
    *\* – needed for communication purposes*
* **Summary**  
  Provide a 20-30 words summary of the article that can be used for links.
* **Length of the Content**  
  Submit articles that are between 500-750 words. Please submit final drafts that have proofread, and grammar and spelling checked.  
  Do not include the graphics or photos within the document. It should be saved separately in the same folder where the file resides.
* **Formatting**  
  Send your document at **biron.neilmarc@gordoncollege.edu.ph** with the following format details:
  + Subject : Request for Posting (Website)
  + Body : Author’s Information
  + Topic : Event/ Celebration
  + Title : Sportsfest
  + Author’s Full name : Juan Dela Cruz
  + Author’s Phone Number : 607.7175
  + Author’s Email Address : juandelacruz@gmail.com
  + Department : College of Allied Health Studies
  + Summary of your article.
  + Attachment:
    - document file of your article
    - picture1.jpg\* / picture1.png\* / picture1.svg\*  
      *\* – you can select one particular image type*
* **Links**  
  Provide a list of referenced links in the text or at the end of the submitted article. The committee will review these links to determine if they are appropriate for the website and will determine if they will be included or not (especially for copyright acknowledgment).
* **Categorization of Submission**  
  Articles that will be posted in the website can be categorized into;
  + News
  + Announcement or Guidelines
  + Notice or Advisory
* **Tags**  
  This will be use to sub-categorized your submission;
  + CAHS *(College of Allied Health Studies)*
  + CCS *(College of Computer Studies)*
  + CBA *(College of Business and Accountancy)*
  + CEAS *(College of Education, Arts, and Sciences)*
  + CHTM *(College of Hospitality and Tourism Management)*
  + IGS (*Institute of Graduate Studies)*
  + OSWS*(Office of Student Welfare and Services)*
  + Campus
  + Alumni
  + Administration
  + Academics
  + Forefront
### Content Administration
All web content submitted must be approved prior to posting. The following individuals retain the right to edit or request changes:
* College President
* Website Administrator
* Head of ICSU
* Vice Presidents
* Requestor/Author of the Content
### Website Content Update/ Revision
Thou postings are checked for spelling, grammar and factual accuracy, there will be some instances that can result unwanted errors.
The GC Website Committee will allow update or revision of articles posted on the website for the following reasons;
* Missing or Duplicate Titles
* Misspelled names
* Overlooked misspelled words or grammatical errors
* Missing images or links
* Missing details or information
### Website Content Removal
The GC Website Committee reserves the right to remove any posted content that contains copyrighted materials which include, but not limited to: text, images, graphics, sound recordings, video recordings or software programs.

# CEAS FINAL COACHING FOR IN-HOUSE BLEPT REVIEW 2026
[Image: CEAS FINAL COACHING FOR IN-HOUSE BLEPT REVIEW 2026]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Tuesday, May 12, 2026**
The Gordon College – College of Education, Arts and Sciences (CEAS) successfully conducted the FINAL COACHING FOR IN-HOUSE BLEPT REVIEW 2026 at SM Olongapo Downtown Cinema 3 on May 11, 2026.
The event formally opened with the opening remarks of Dr. Eder E. Magsayo, Dean of CEAS, followed by special messages from Dr. Darwin P. Paguio, Vice President for Academic Affairs, and Dr. Ediric D. Gadia, Vice President for Research, Development, and Community Extension Services. The activity was also attended and supported by the College President, Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano, who delivered an inspiring message to all future educators.
Mr. Kristoffer Paul C. Obispo, Program Chair – BSED Science, introduced the Review Master, Dr. Brenda B. Corpuz, whose expertise and dedication continue to inspire future educators in their journey toward excellence.
This activity reflects Gordon College’s unwavering commitment to producing competent, passionate, and board-ready educators equipped to make a difference in the field of education.

# GC RECOGINZED AS INTERNATIONALIZATION CHAMPION (ICONS) FOR ITS PERFORMANCE IN THE TIMES HIGHER EDUCATION IMPACT RANKINGS 2025
[Image: GC RECOGINZED AS INTERNATIONALIZATION CHAMPION (ICONS) FOR ITS PERFORMANCE IN THE TIMES HIGHER EDUCATION IMPACT RANKINGS 2025]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, December 10, 2025**
Today, the College was honored by the Commission on Higher Education for our outstanding achievements in global rankings, sustainability, ASEAN awareness, and internationalization during the Internationalization Champions of Nation-Building and Sustainability (ICONS) Awards 2025, held on December 10, 2025 (Wednesday), from 1:00 PM to 9:00 PM at the Philippine International Convention Center (PICC) in Pasay City.
The College was recognized for its high-impact sustainability initiatives, commendable performance in international rankings and ratings, pioneering efforts to advance internationalization, and valuable contributions to United Nation’s Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs).
This recognition is the result of our Internationalization initiative, based on the released ranking results for this year in the Times Higher Education Impact Rankings 2025.
The College extends its gratitude to Hon. Rolen C. Paulino Jr., City Mayor; Hon. Cristabelle Marie C. Paulino, Committee on Education Chairperson; Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano, College President; Dr. Kristin Joy A. Mendoza, Vice President for Institutional Planning and External Affairs; Ms. Precious S. De Castro, Coordinator of the International Affairs Unit; other Vice Presidents, BOT Members, College Officials, Management Council Members, faculty members, administrative personnel, and all other stakeholders who contributed to this achievement.

# CHTM Sharks Roar at 2nd CHTM Rally 2025
[Image: CHTM Sharks Roar at 2nd CHTM Rally 2025]
[CHTM](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/chtm/) [News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Thursday, September 11, 2025**
GORDON COLLEGE — The College of Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM) filled the East Tapinac Covered Court with cheers and chants as the 2nd CHTM Rally unfolded last September 9, 2025, igniting the spirit of the CHTM Sharks in preparation for the much-anticipated Siklab 2025 Sportsfest on September 15–19, 2025.
The event showcased the final lineup of the College of Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM) official teams and players.
Furthermore, athletes from the CHTM Sharks introduced themselves through lively intermission numbers performed by each sports team, adding excitement and entertainment to the rally.
Player representatives from each team also participated in a brief interview with the Masters of Ceremony, where they shared their thoughts and enthusiasm for the upcoming Siklab competition.
Moreover, the event was organized in collaboration with the College of Hospitality and Tourism Management Student Council (CHTM-SC), Arapaap Productions, the Junior Association of Hospitality Management Students (JAHMS), and the League of Tourism Students of the Philippines (LTSP), who worked together to ensure the program’s success.
The College of Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM) Dean, Dr. Mark Alvin Lazaro, Assistant Dean Vincent Dela Cruz, Bachelor of Science and Tourism Management (BSTM) Program Coordinator Ma’am Lizette Ann P. Sia, Bachelor of Science in Hospitality Management (BSHM) Coordinator Vince Richard Gomez, and ARAPAAP Adviser Ms. Jenny Mae M. Lagman also extended strong support and leadership, which greatly guided the preparations for the rally.
To cap off the event, the atmosphere reached its peak as the CHTM Sharks filled the covered court with their department’s official Siklab cheer, embodying their spirit of unity and readiness for the upcoming sportsfest.| via Lovely Doroin/The FOREFRONT

# World University Rankings 2026: GC Recognized as Reporter Institution
[Image: World University Rankings 2026: GC Recognized as Reporter Institution]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Saturday, October 18, 2025**
Gordon College has officially been granted Reporter Status in the recent Times Higher Education (THE) World University Rankings 2026, released last October 9.
The college is among six Higher Education Institutions (HEIs) in Central Luzon that have been granted this recognition, including:  
– Bulacan State University  
– Central Luzon State University  
– Nueva Ecija University of Science and Technology  
– Tarlac Agricultural University, and  
– City College of San Fernando Pampanga
The Times Higher Education annually releases their World University Rankings that measure the performance of universities in eighteen (18) performance indicators across five core pillars: teaching, research environment, research quality, international outlook, and industry. A reporter status is granted to HEIs who have submitted relevant data but did not meet eligibility criteria to receive a rank. Inclusion in the reporter status allows HEIs to receive feedback in their performance across the metrics to support future decision making.
Meanwhile, Ateneo de Manila University is the highest ranking university in the Philippines with a banded rank of 1001-1200, followed by the University of the Philippines (1201-1500), De la Salle University (1501+), Mapua University (1501+). Mindanao State University – Iligan Institute of Technology (1501+), and University of Santo Tomas (1501+). The University of Oxford remains to be the top global ranking university for the tenth consecutive year. | via Lance Isaac Leon/The FOREFRONT
Sources: Times Higher Education World University Rankings 2026 and CHED Regional Office III (via Facebook)

# CCTV Privacy and Consent Notices
[Image: CCTV Privacy and Consent Notices]
[Alumni](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/alumni/) [LIMC](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/tag/limc/)
**Monday, January 2, 2023**
To all stakeholders of Gordon College, please be informed that the Closed-Circuit Television (CCTV) cameras installed in the main building and premises of the college are used to process personal information, as defined by Republic Act 10173 or the Data Privacy act of 2012. These cameras capture and record the identities of individuals within their scope and are primarily intended for security and safety purposes. As mandated by the law, we would like to remind you that your consent is required for the processing of your personal information captured by these CCTV cameras. Your continued presence within the college premises is considered an implied consent to the processing of your personal information by the CCTV cameras. Rest assured that all personal information captured by the CCTV cameras will be treated with the utmost confidentiality and will only be accessed by authorized personnel for legitimate purposes. Thank you for your cooperation in ensuring compliance with the Data Privacy act of 2012.

# Empowering Solo Parents: Gordon College Launches ‘Weave of Strength’ Initiative
[Image: Empowering Solo Parents: Gordon College Launches ‘Weave of Strength’ Initiative]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, April 8, 2026**
Empowering communities through sustainable livelihood and inclusive development, Weave of Strength: Turning Scraps into Sustainable Livelihood was conducted on April 7, 2026 at the Gordon College Function Hall. The initiative provided solo parents and economically disadvantaged individuals with practical skills in transforming recycled fabric scraps into sustainable sources of income.
The program featured an inspiring message from College President Dr. Imelda DP. Soriano and the support of Vice President for RDCES, Dr. Ediric D. Gadia, reinforcing the college’s commitment to community service and development.
With the support of the Rotary Club of Downtown Olongapo, and through the coordinated efforts of RDCES and the College of Hospitality and Tourism Management as the implementing unit, the initiative reflects Gordon College’s core values of Character, Excellence, and Service.
To ensure long-term sustainability, the initiative adopts a capacity-building approach by equipping administrative personnel to serve as future facilitators, enabling continuous implementation and expanded community reach.

# CEAS Mentors Duo Reclaims Gold, Overpowers CBA in Mixed Doubles Badminton Clash
[Image: CEAS Mentors Duo Reclaims Gold, Overpowers CBA in Mixed Doubles Badminton Clash]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, September 17, 2025**
TRICKSHOT COVERED COURT—In a smashing display of speed, precision, and teamwork, the College of Education, Arts and Sciences (CEAS) Mentors reclaimed badminton glory, dominating the mixed doubles championship against the College of Business and Accountancy (CBA) Wizards on September 16, 2025, at the Trickshot Badminton Court.
Right from the opening serve, the dynamic duo of Daniel Ruzzel Bernal and Mary Anne Lafuente wasted no time asserting their dominance with lightning-quick reflexes and razor-sharp shot placements.
Consequently, their rapid-fire rallies and clever net play kept the CBA Wizards scrambling across the court, ending the first set in convincing fashion at 21-9, with CEAS clearly in control.
Building on that first-set momentum, Bernal and Lafuente came out swinging in the second set, smashing their way to an early lead through seamless communication and smart positioning.
Meanwhile, although the Wizards attempted a comeback with aggressive clears and pushes, every move was countered by the Mentors’ calculated drop shots, steady defense, and powerful smashes, closing the set at 21-1 to secure the championship crown.
After the final rally, Lafuente emphasized that the victory was not only a triumph of skill but also a testament to their trust in each other, both on and off the court.
Similarly, Bernal expressed his pride and excitement after clinching the gold, saying, “Sobrang saya po and super proud po ako sa partner ko.”
Before reaching the finals, the duo battled past the CHTM Sharks in the elimination round, overcoming sharp smashes and relentless returns to secure a 30–10 win and a spot in the championship stage.
Furthermore, the CEAS pair showed that age and experience were no barriers, as their first match as college students turned into a golden opportunity to prove their fire and determination. | via Lovely Mae M. Doroin/The FOREFRONTFOREFRONT

# Empowering GFPS members through the Application of Harmonized Gender and Development Guidelines
[Image: Empowering GFPS members through the Application of Harmonized Gender and Development Guidelines]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Tuesday, June 17, 2025**
The Gordon College, through its Diversity, Equity, and Inclusion Unit as the GC-GFPS Secretariat, successfully facilitated a seminar for members of the Gordon College Gender and Development Focal Point System (GC-GFPS) entitled “Empowering GFPS Members through the Application of the Harmonized Gender and Development Guidelines” on June 13, 2025, held at the GC Library Audio-Visual Room.
This capacity-building initiative was organized to further strengthen the College’s gender mainstreaming efforts. It aimed to equip GC-GFPS members with the essential knowledge and practical skills needed to effectively integrate gender perspectives into the institution’s programs, projects, and activities. The seminar primarily focused on the application of the Harmonized Gender and Development Guidelines (HGDG)—a critical framework designed to ensure that development initiatives are gender-responsive and aligned with the overarching goals of gender equality and women’s empowerment.

# Six Central Luzon HEIs Attain Reporter Status in THE Asia University Rankings 2026
[Image: Six Central Luzon HEIs Attain Reporter Status in THE Asia University Rankings 2026]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Wednesday, May 6, 2026**
Central Luzon continues to gain ground in the global higher education landscape as six (6) Higher Education Institutions from the region have attained Reporter Status in the Times Higher Education (THE) Asia University Rankings 2026—doubling from three (3) HEIs in 2025.
This significant increase reflects the region’s steady progress in academic visibility, institutional development, and international engagement. It also signals that more HEIs in Central Luzon are stepping onto the global stage and contributing to the broader discourse of higher education in Asia.
This achievement directly aligns with the Commission’s ACHIEVE Agenda, particularly Inclusive and Impact-Driven Internationalization, underscoring the commitment of Philippine higher education institutions to expand global linkages and strengthen international competitiveness.
CHEDRO III commends these institutions for this noteworthy milestone and encourages all HEIs in the region to continue striving for excellence and thriving in the international landscape. This achievement marks a meaningful step forward in strengthening Region III’s presence and recognition worldwide.

# Beauty Meets Sportsmanship: Pislaan and Pradas Crowned as Mr. and Ms. Sportsfest 2025
[Image: Beauty Meets Sportsmanship: Pislaan and Pradas Crowned as Mr. and Ms. Sportsfest 2025]
[News](https://gordoncollege.edu.ph/w3/category/news/)
**Monday, September 15, 2025**
GORDON COLLEGE, OLONGAPO CITY— Cheers filled the East Tapinac Oval Grounds as the perfect blend of charm and athletic spirit came to life with representatives from various colleges showcasing their poise and sportsmanship, on the recently concluded Mr. and Ms. Sportsfest 2025 on Monday, September 15.
Kick-starting off the event with a bang, representatives wowed the crowd with their charisma and creative display of athletic-themed fashion for the Best in Sportswear award, which went to Mr. Chester Pislaan and Ms. Bhavjeet Kaur L. Singh from the College of Education, Arts and Sciences (CEAS) Mentors.
But the night didn’t end there—the battle for the ultimate title pushed the candidates to bring out their best poise, personality, and confidence. As the event drew to a close, the stage erupted in cheers and anticipation as the overall winners were finally announced.
After an intense showdown, Ms. Dhana Del C. Pradas from the College of Business and Accountancy (CBA) Wizards and Mr. Chester Pislaan from the College of Education, Arts and Sciences (CEAS) emerged as Mr. and Ms. Sportsfest—proving that talent, wit, and stage presence shine brightest when matched with determination and sportsmanship.
Furthermore, hot on their heels, the 1st Runner-Up titles went to Mr. Marc Anthony Acula (CBA) Wizards and Ms. Bhavjeet Kaur L. Singh (CEAS) Mentors, whose outstanding performances made them strong contenders throughout the competition.
Meanwhile, Mr. Krizztan Vic A. Dominico and Ms. Ron Eriele Joi P. Agustin from College of Allied Health Studies (CAHS) Tigers secured the 2nd Runner-Up spot, earning admiration for their dynamic partnership.
In 3rd place, Mr. Charles De Leon from College of Computer Studies (CCS) Phoenix and Ms. Alexandra Macky Alisbo from College of Hospitality and Tourism Management (CHTM) claimed their rightful spot. At the same time, Mr. Jack Francis P. Meran (CHTM) Sharks and Ms. Janna Lyn Rolls, CCS Phoenix, completed the roster as the 4th place finishers. | via Love Lorainne Venus Acedo/The FOREFRONT
